The rewriting reveals the entropy’s formal similarity to the complexity relative
entropy
(
12
)
.

Proposition 20

(Alternative expression for hypothesis-testing relative entropy)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state; and
\Gamma
, any positive-semidefinite operator.
Let
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
.
The hypothesis-testing relative entropy obeys

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{H}}^{\eta}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)

\displaystyle=-\log\left({\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leqslant Q\leqslant\mathds{1}\\
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta\end{subarray}}\left\{{\frac{\tr\left({Q\Gamma}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)}}\right\}}\right)\ .

(93)

Without loss of generality, we can assume that every candidate effect
Q
for the optimization satisfies
\lVert{Q}\rVert=1
.
If a candidate
Q
did not, we could replace
Q
with a candidate
Q^{\prime}\coloneqq Q/\lVert{Q}\rVert\geqslant Q
that achieves the same objective value as
Q
.

Proof.

Let
Q
denote any POVM effect such that
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta
.
Let
Q^{\prime}\coloneqq\eta Q/\tr({Q\rho})
.
By definition,
Q^{\prime}
satisfies
\tr({Q^{\prime}\rho})=\eta
.

Q
and
Q^{\prime}
are candidates for the
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{\eta}
optimization in
(
90
)
and achieve the same objective value:

\displaystyle\frac{\tr\left({Q\Gamma}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)}=\frac{\tr\left({Q^{\prime}\Gamma}\right)}{\tr\left({Q^{\prime}\rho}\right)}\ .

(94)

Consequently, the set of candidate objective values in
(
93
)
is unaltered if we restrict to POVM effects
Q^{\prime}
satisfying
\tr({Q^{\prime}\rho})=\eta
:

\displaystyle\left\{{\frac{\tr\left({Q\Gamma}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)}:0\leqslant Q\leqslant\mathds{1},\,\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)\geqslant\eta}\right\}=\left\{{\frac{\tr({Q^{\prime}\Gamma})}{\tr\left({Q^{\prime}\rho}\right)}:0\leqslant Q^{\prime}\leqslant\mathds{1},\,\tr\left({Q^{\prime}\rho}\right)=\eta}\right\}\ .

(95)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leqslant Q\leqslant\mathds{1}\\
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta\end{subarray}}\left\{{\frac{\tr\left({Q\Gamma}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)}}\right\}\ =\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leqslant Q^{\prime}\leqslant\mathds{1}\\
\tr({Q^{\prime}\rho})=\eta\end{subarray}}\left\{{\frac{\tr\left({Q^{\prime}\Gamma}\right)}{\tr\left({Q^{\prime}\rho}\right)}}\right\}\ =\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leqslant Q^{\prime}\leqslant\mathds{1}\\
\tr({Q^{\prime}\rho})=\eta\end{subarray}}\left\{{\frac{\tr\left({Q^{\prime}\Gamma}\right)}{\eta}}\right\}\ .

(96)

The second equality follows because
\tr({Q^{\prime}\rho})=\eta
for every
Q^{\prime}
in the second infimum’s domain.

Consider, again, any effect
Q
satisfying
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta
and the effect
Q^{\prime}\coloneqq\eta Q/\tr({Q\rho})
.
Clearly
Q^{\prime}\leqslant Q
, so
\tr({Q^{\prime}\Gamma})/\eta\leqslant\tr({Q\Gamma})/\eta
.
Hence, the final infimum in
(
96
)
does not decrease if we extend its domain to all
Q
satisfying
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta
:

\displaystyle\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leqslant Q^{\prime}\leqslant\mathds{1}\\
\tr({Q^{\prime}\rho})=\eta\end{subarray}}\left\{{\frac{\tr\left({Q^{\prime}\Gamma}\right)}{\eta}}\right\}\,=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leqslant Q\leqslant\mathds{1}\\
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta\end{subarray}}\left\{{\frac{\tr\left({Q\Gamma}\right)}{\eta}}\right\}\ .

(97)

Finally, the second infimum in
(
97
)
is a minimum, since the infimum’s domain is compact.
Hence,

\displaystyle\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leqslant Q\leqslant\mathds{1}\\
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta\end{subarray}}\left\{{\frac{\tr\left({Q\Gamma}\right)}{\eta}}\right\}\,=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leqslant Q\leqslant\mathds{1}\\
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta\end{subarray}}\left\{{\frac{\tr\left({Q\Gamma}\right)}{\eta}}\right\}\,=e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{\eta}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)}\ .

(98)

The second equality follows from
Appendix

C
.
Chaining together
(
96
)
,
(
97
)
, and
(
98
)
yields an equality equivalent to
(
93
)
.
∎

Appendix D
Complexity (relative) entropy and its variants

Imagine a hypothesis test performed by an observer able to render only limited-complexity measurement effects.
The hypothesis-testing relative entropy is ill-suited for such a test: the optimization in
(
90
)
is appropriate only for an observer who can render all POVM effects.
In
Section

D.1
, we introduce the complexity (relative) entropy as a hypothesis-testing (relative) entropy tailored for the complexity-limited observer.
In the optimization defining the complexity (relative) entropy, candidate POVM effects cannot exceed a given complexity
r\geqslant 0
.

Section

D.2
details the complexity (relative) entropy’s elementary properties.
In
Section

D.3
, we apply the complexity relative entropy to hypothesis testing with complexity limitations.
In
Section

D.4
, we bound the complexity (relative) entropy using state-complexity measures.

Section

D.5
mainly concerns a variant of the complexity (relative) entropy, the reduced complexity (relative) entropy.
The reduced complexity entropy features in our data-compression results and appears in Ref.
YungerHalpern_2022_Uncomplexity
as “the complexity entropy.”
Last, in
Section

D.6
, we define the complexity conditional entropy and study some of its elementary properties.
In the following,
\{\mathsf{M}^{r}\}
denotes any family of POVM-effect-complexity sets (
Section

B.3
).
The complexity (relative) entropy and its variants are defined with respect to this set, unless further specified.

D.1
Definition of the complexity (relative) entropy

Definition 21

(Complexity relative entropy)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state; and
\Gamma
, any positive-semidefinite operator.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
.
Here,
\rho
,
\Gamma
, and every
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
act on the same Hilbert space.
The
complexity-restricted hypothesis-testing relative entropy
, or simply the
complexity relative entropy
, is

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)\coloneqq-\log\;\left({\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}\\
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta\end{subarray}}\left\{{\frac{\tr\left({Q\Gamma}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)}}\right\}}\right)\ .

(99)

The denominator
\tr({Q\rho})
is a normalization factor ensuring that the complexity (relative) entropy has
some desired properties.
First, the complexity relative entropy satisfies
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\rho)=0
(
Section

D.2
).
Second, if
\rho
acts on a Hilbert space of dimensionality
d
, the complexity entropy assumes values in the range
[0,\log(d)]
(
Section

D.2
).
The hypothesis-testing relative entropy is the same with a denominator
\tr({Q\rho})
[as in
(
93
)
] or
\eta
[as in
(
90
)
].
In contrast, the complexity relative entropy might increase if we replace
\tr({Q\rho})
with
\eta
in
(
99
)
: it may be impossible to find a
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
such that
\tr({Q\rho})=\eta
[or such that
\tr({Q\rho})
approximates
\eta
arbitrarily well].
This is typically the case, for instance, for a family of sets that contains only projectors; such a family is defined by
(
11
)
.

Definition 22

(Complexity entropy)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
.
The
complexity-restricted hypothesis-testing entropy
, or simply the
complexity entropy
, is

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\coloneqq-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\mathds{1})=\log\;\left({\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}\\
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta\end{subarray}}\left\{{\frac{\tr\left({Q}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)}}\right\}}\right)\ .

(100)

The complexity entropy measures how well one can distinguish
\rho
from the maximally mixed state, using a limited-complexity measurement effect.
Indeed, if
\rho
acts on a Hilbert space of dimensionality
d
, then
H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)=\log({d})-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\pi)
. The
\pi\coloneqq\mathds{1}/d
denotes the maximally mixed state.
Two special cases offer insight. First,
H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)=0
if
\rho
is pure and
\rho\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
.
Second,
H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)=\log(d)
if
\rho
is normalized and
\mathds{1}\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
is the only candidate for the
H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)
optimization.

The complexity entropy is analogous to the strong complexity of Ref.
Brandão et al. 2021
.
Compared to the strong complexity, the complexity entropy quantifies distinguishability in terms of a hypothesis test, rather than a modified trace distance.
Also, the complexity entropy bears a meaningful interpretation for mixed states.

D.2
Elementary properties of the complexity (relative) entropy

The complexity (relative) entropy possesses several basic properties.

Proposition 23

(Vanishing complexity relative entropy)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
.
It holds that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\rho)=0\ .

(101)

Proof.

Every candidate effect
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
for the optimization in
(
99
)
, including
\mathds{1}\in\mathsf{M}^{r=0}\subset\mathsf{M}^{r}
, achieves the objective value
\tr({Q\rho})/\tr({Q\rho})=1
.
Hence,
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\rho)=\log(1)=0
.
∎

In general, the converse is false: there may exist a state
\sigma\neq\rho
such that
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)=0
.
This situation arises if the measurement effects in
\mathsf{M}^{r}
are too crude to distinguish
\rho
from
\sigma
.
For instance, consider the sets
\mathsf{M}^{r}
defined in
(
11
)
for
n
qubits, with respect to the set of two-qubit gates.
Let
\rho=\lvert{1^{n}}\rangle\mkern-1.8mu\langle{1^{n}}\rvert
.
For any
\eta\in(0,1]
, the only effect
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r=0}
satisfying
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta
is
Q=\mathds{1}
.
Therefore, trivially,
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r=0},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)=0
for all
\sigma
.

The complexity relative entropy
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
lies within a fixed range dependent on only
\tr(\rho)
and the eigenvalues of
\Gamma
.
The range takes a simple form if
\rho
is normalized and
\Gamma
is positive-definite.

Proposition 24

(General bounds)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state, and
\Gamma
any positive-semidefinite operator, that act on a Hilbert space of dimensionality
d
.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
.
It holds that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)

\displaystyle\geqslant-\log\bm{(}\tr({\Gamma})\bm{)}+\log\bm{(}\tr({\rho})\bm{)}\ .

(102a)

Furthermore, if
\Gamma
is positive-definite (has full rank),

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)

\displaystyle\leqslant\log\left({\lVert{\Gamma^{-1}}\rVert}\right)+\log\bm{(}\tr({\rho})\bm{)}\ .

(102b)

In particular,

\displaystyle-\log\bm{(}\tr({\rho})\bm{)}\leqslant H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\leqslant\log(d)-\log\bm{(}\tr({\rho})\bm{)}\ .

(103)

Consequently, if
\rho
is normalized and
\Gamma
is positive-definite,

\displaystyle-\log\bm{(}\tr({\Gamma})\bm{)}\leqslant D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)

\displaystyle\leqslant\log\left({\lVert{\Gamma^{-1}}\rVert}\right)\ ,

(104a)

\displaystyle\text{and}\;\;\;0\leqslant H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)

\displaystyle\leqslant\log(d)\ .

(104b)

Eq.

102a
implies that
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)\geqslant 0
for normalized states
\rho
and
\sigma
.

Proof.

(
102a
)
follows because
\mathds{1}\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
is a candidate effect for the
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
optimization.
To prove
(
102b
)
, let
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
denote any candidate for the
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
optimization.
If
\Gamma
is positive-definite,
\Gamma\geqslant\lVert{\Gamma^{-1}}\rVert^{-1}\mathds{1}
, wherein
\lVert{\Gamma^{-1}}\rVert^{-1}
is the smallest eigenvalue of
\Gamma
.
Furthermore,
\rho\leqslant\tr({\rho})\mathds{1}
, since
\tr({\rho})\leqslant 1
.
Thus,
\tr({Q\Gamma})\geqslant\lVert{\Gamma^{-1}}\rVert^{-1}\tr({Q})
, and
\tr({Q\rho})\leqslant\tr({\rho})\,\tr({Q})
.
These two inequalities, together, imply that

\displaystyle\frac{\tr\left({Q\Gamma}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)}\ \geqslant\frac{\lVert{\Gamma^{-1}}\rVert^{-1}\tr\left({Q}\right)}{\tr\left({\rho}\right)\tr\left({Q}\right)}\ =\bm{(}\lVert{\Gamma^{-1}}\rVert\,\tr({\rho})\bm{)}^{-1}\ .

(105)

Therefore,
\bm{(}\lVert{\Gamma^{-1}}\rVert\,\tr({\rho})\bm{)}^{-1}
lower-bounds the objective value
\tr({Q\Gamma})/\tr({Q\rho})
of every candidate effect
Q
.
By the definition of an infimum,

\displaystyle\bm{(}\lVert{\Gamma^{-1}}\rVert\,\tr({\rho})\bm{)}^{-1}\leqslant\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}\\
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta\end{subarray}}\left\{{\frac{\tr\left({Q\Gamma}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)}}\right\}=e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)}\ ,

(106)

which is equivalent to
(
102b
)
.
One obtains
(
103
)
by setting
\Gamma=\mathds{1}
in
(
102a
)
and
(
102b
)
.
∎

The complexity relative entropy never exceeds the hypothesis-testing relative entropy.

Proposition 25

(Upper bound by hypothesis-testing relative entropy)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state; and
\Gamma
, any positive-semidefinite operator.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
.
It holds that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)\leqslant D_{\mathrm{H}}^{\eta}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)\;\;\;\text{and}\;\;\;H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\geqslant H_{\mathrm{H}}^{\eta}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\ .

(107)

Proof.

The
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{\eta}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
optimization ranges over all POVM effects, while the
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
optimization ranges over only effects in
\mathsf{M}^{r}
.
∎

The greater an agent’s computational power (the greater the
r
), the less mixed
\rho
appears
[
the less
H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)
is
]
.
Likewise, the greater an agent’s error intolerance (the greater the
\eta
), the more mixed
\rho
appears
[
the greater
H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)
is
]
.
Consequently, the complexity entropy monotonically decreases as
r
increases and monotonically increases as
\eta
increases.
The complexity entropy inherits its monotonicity in
\eta
from the hypothesis-testing entropy.

Proposition 26

(Monotonicity in
r
and
\eta
)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state; and
\Gamma
, any positive-semidefinite operator.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
.

1.

For all
r^{\prime}\geqslant r
,
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r^{\prime}},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)\geqslant D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
, and
H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r^{\prime}},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\leqslant H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)
.

2.

For all
\eta^{\prime}\in(0,\eta]
,
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta^{\prime}}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)\geqslant D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
, and
H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta^{\prime}}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\leqslant H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)
.

Proof.

The monotonicity in
r
follows because
\mathsf{M}^{r}\subset\mathsf{M}^{r^{\prime}}
: every candidate
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
for the
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
optimization belongs to
\mathsf{M}^{r^{\prime}}
and is therefore a candidate for the
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r^{\prime}},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
optimization.
The monotonicity in
\eta
follows because every candidate
Q
for the
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
optimization satisfies
\tr(Q\rho)\geqslant\eta\geqslant\eta^{\prime}
and is therefore a candidate for the
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta^{\prime}}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
optimization.
∎

Like standard relative entropies, the complexity relative entropy enjoys a scaling property in its second argument.

Proposition 27

(Scaling property in the second argument)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state; and
\Gamma
, any positive-semidefinite operator.
Let
r\geqslant 0
,
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
, and
a>0
.
It holds that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}a\Gamma)=D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)-\log(a)\ .

(108)

Proof.

The equality holds because
\log(a)
factorizes out of the optimizer in
(
99
)
.
∎

The complexity (relative) entropy inverts (preserves) the partial order of positive-semidefinite operators.

Proposition 28

(Monotonicity under operator ordering)
.

Let
\rho
and
\rho^{\prime}\geqslant\rho
denote any subnormalized states.
Let
\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}\leqslant\Gamma
denote any positive-semidefinite operators.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
.
It holds that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)

\displaystyle\leqslant D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho^{\prime}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma^{\prime})

(109a)

\displaystyle\text{and}\;\;\;H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)

\displaystyle\geqslant H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho^{\prime}-NoValue-)\ .

(109b)

Proof.

For every POVM effect
Q
,
\tr({Q\rho})\leqslant\tr({Q\rho^{\prime}})
, and
\tr({Q\Gamma})\geqslant\tr({Q\Gamma^{\prime}})
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)}=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}\\
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta\end{subarray}}\left\{{\frac{\tr\left({Q\Gamma}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)}}\right\}\geqslant\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}\\
\tr({Q\rho^{\prime}})\geqslant\eta\end{subarray}}\left\{{\frac{\tr\left({Q\Gamma^{\prime}}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\rho^{\prime}}\right)}}\right\}=e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho^{\prime}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma^{\prime})}\ .

(110)

One obtains
(
109b
)
by setting
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}=\mathds{1}
in
(
109a
)
.
∎

For tensor-product states, the complexity entropy has a property similar to the von Neumann entropy’s subadditivity.

Proposition 29

(Subadditivity for tensor-product states)
.

Let
S
and
S^{\prime}
denote distinct quantum systems.
Let
\rho_{S}
and
\rho_{S^{\prime}}^{\prime}
denote any subnormalized states, and let
\Gamma_{S}
and
\Gamma_{S^{\prime}}^{\prime}
denote any positive-semidefinite operators.
Let
r,r^{\prime}\geqslant 0
,
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
, and
\eta^{\prime}\in(0,\tr(\rho^{\prime})]
.
It holds that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r+r^{\prime}},\,{\eta\eta^{\prime}}}(\rho\otimes\rho^{\prime}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma\otimes\Gamma^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geqslant D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)+D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r^{\prime}},\,{\eta^{\prime}}}(\rho^{\prime}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma^{\prime})

(111a)

\displaystyle\text{and}\;\;\;H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r+r^{\prime}},\,{\eta\eta^{\prime}}}-NoValue-(\rho\otimes\rho^{\prime}-NoValue-)

\displaystyle\leqslant H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)+H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r^{\prime}},\,{\eta^{\prime}}}-NoValue-(\rho^{\prime}-NoValue-)\ .

(111b)

A consequence of
Section

D.2
is, ancillas cannot decrease the complexity relative entropy. For all subnormalized states
\rho
and
\sigma
defined on the same Hilbert space, and for all subnormalized states
\tau
,

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\otimes\tau\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma\otimes\tau)\geqslant D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)\ .

(112)

Inequality
(
112
)
follows from setting
r^{\prime}=0
and
\eta^{\prime}=1
in
(
111a
)
and applying
Section

D.2

\big[{D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r^{\prime}},\,{\eta^{\prime}}}(\tau\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\tau)=0}\big]
.
Inequality
(
112
)
is compatible with observations about the power of the one-clean-qubit computational model (DQC1)
Knill_98_Power
and about how tossing an extra pure qubit into a black hole is expected to decrease the black hole’s complexity
Brown_18_Second
;
Susskind 2018
.

Proof of
subsection D.2
.

Consider any
\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}>0
.
By the definitions of
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
and
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r^{\prime}},\,{\eta^{\prime}}}(\rho^{\prime}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma^{\prime})
, there exist
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}_{S}
, with
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta
, and
Q^{\prime}\in\mathsf{M}^{r^{\prime}}_{S^{\prime}}
, with
\tr({Q^{\prime}\rho^{\prime}})\geqslant\eta^{\prime}
, such that

\displaystyle\frac{\tr\left({Q\Gamma}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)}\leqslant e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)}+\zeta\;\;\;\;\;\;\text{and}\;\;\;\;\;\;\frac{\tr\left({Q^{\prime}\Gamma^{\prime}}\right)}{\tr\left({Q^{\prime}\rho^{\prime}}\right)}\leqslant e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r^{\prime}},\,{\eta^{\prime}}}(\rho^{\prime}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma^{\prime})}+\zeta^{\prime}\ .

(113)

By
Section

B.3
,
\mathsf{M}^{r}_{S}\otimes\mathsf{M}^{r^{\prime}}_{S^{\prime}}\subseteq\mathsf{M}^{r+r^{\prime}}_{SS^{\prime}}
, so
Q\otimes Q^{\prime}\in\mathsf{M}^{r+r^{\prime}}_{SS^{\prime}}
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\tr\left({[{Q\otimes Q^{\prime}}]\,[{\rho\otimes\rho^{\prime}}]}\right)=\tr({Q\rho})\tr({Q^{\prime}\rho^{\prime}})\geqslant\eta\eta^{\prime}\ .

(114)

Therefore,
Q\otimes Q^{\prime}
is a candidate for the
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r+r^{\prime}},\,{\eta\eta^{\prime}}}(\rho\otimes\rho^{\prime}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma\otimes\Gamma^{\prime})
optimization.
Consequently,

\displaystyle e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r+r^{\prime}},\,{\eta\eta^{\prime}}}(\rho\otimes\rho^{\prime}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma\otimes\Gamma^{\prime})}\leqslant\frac{\tr\left({Q\Gamma}\right)\tr\left({Q^{\prime}\Gamma^{\prime}}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)\tr\left({Q^{\prime}\rho^{\prime}}\right)}\leqslant\Big({e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)}+\zeta}\Big)\Big({e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r^{\prime}},\,{\eta^{\prime}}}(\rho^{\prime}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma^{\prime})}+\zeta^{\prime}}\Big)\ ,

(115)

which implies
(
111a
)
, since
\zeta
and
\zeta^{\prime}
are arbitrary.
One obtains
(
111b
)
by setting
\Gamma=\mathds{1}_{S}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\mathds{1}_{S^{\prime}}
in
(
111a
)
.
∎

The complexity relative entropy never increases under any partial trace.

Proposition 30

(Monotonicity under partial traces)
.

Let
A
and
B
denote distinct quantum systems.
Let
\rho_{AB}
denote any subnormalized quantum state of
AB
.
Let
\Gamma_{AB}
denote any positive-semidefinite operator.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
.
It holds that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{AB}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma_{AB})\geqslant D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{A}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma_{A})\ ,

(116)

wherein
\rho_{A}\coloneqq\tr_{B}({\rho_{AB}})
and
\Gamma_{A}\coloneqq\tr_{B}({\Gamma_{AB}})
.
Furthermore,

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho_{AB}-NoValue-)\leqslant H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho_{A}-NoValue-)+\log(d_{B})\ .

(117)

Proof.

Consider any
\zeta>0
.
There exists a
Q_{A}\in\mathsf{M}^{r}_{A}
such that
\tr({Q_{A}\rho_{A}})\geqslant\eta
and
\tr({Q_{A}\Gamma_{A}})/\tr({Q_{A}\rho_{A}})\leqslant\exp\bm{(}-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{A}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma_{A})\bm{)}+\zeta
.
By Properties
(i)
and
(iii)
of
Section

B.3
,
\tilde{Q}_{AB}\coloneqq Q_{A}\otimes\mathds{1}_{B}\in\mathsf{M}^{r}_{AB}
.
Moreover,
\tr({\tilde{Q}_{AB}\rho_{AB}})=\tr({Q_{A}\rho_{A}})\geqslant\eta
.
Therefore,
\tilde{Q}_{AB}
is a candidate for the
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{AB}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma_{AB})
optimization.
Consequently,

\displaystyle e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{AB}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma_{AB})}\leqslant\frac{\tr\left({\tilde{Q}_{AB}\Gamma_{AB}}\right)}{\tr\left({\tilde{Q}_{AB}\rho_{AB}}\right)}=\frac{\tr\left({Q_{A}\Gamma_{A}}\right)}{\tr\left({Q_{A}\rho_{A}}\right)}\leqslant e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{A}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma_{A})}+\zeta\ ,

(118)

which implies
(
116
)
, since
\zeta
is arbitrary.

(
117
)
follows from applying first
(
116
)
and then
Section

D.2
, to get

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho_{AB}-NoValue-)=-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{AB}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\mathds{1}_{AB})\leqslant-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{A}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}d_{B}\mathds{1}_{A})=H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho_{A}-NoValue-)+\log(d_{B})\ .

(119)

∎

We can bound the complexity (relative) entropy in some cases where its argument undergoes a limited-complexity unitary.

Proposition 31

(Unitary operations on arguments)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state; and
\Gamma
, any positive-semidefinite operator.
Let
\mathsf{P}
denote any set of simple POVM effects (
Section

B.4
) and
C
any adjoint-invariant unitary-complexity measure (
Section

B.1
).
Let
\{\mathsf{M}^{r}=\mathsf{M}^{r}(\mathsf{P},C)\}
denote the family of POVM-effect-complexity sets defined by
(
87
)
.
Let
r,r^{\prime}\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
.
Let
U
denote any unitary satisfying
C(U)\leqslant r^{\prime}
.
It holds that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r+r^{\prime}},\,{\eta}}(U\rho U^{\dagger}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}U\Gamma U^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\geqslant D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)

(120a)

\displaystyle\text{and}\;\;\;H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r+r^{\prime}},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(U\rho U^{\dagger}-NoValue-)

\displaystyle\leqslant H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\ .

(120b)

Proof.

Consider any
\zeta>0
.
There exists a
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
such that
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta
and
\tr({Q\Gamma})/\tr({Q\rho})\leqslant\exp\bm{(}-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)\bm{)}+\zeta
.

Q=U_{0}^{\dagger}PU_{0}
for some effect
P\in\mathsf{P}
and for some unitary
U_{0}
satisfying
C(U_{0})\leqslant r
.
Let
Q^{\prime}\coloneqq UQU^{\dagger}=(U_{0}U^{\dagger})^{\dagger}P(U_{0}U^{\dagger})
.
By
Section

B.1
and by the adjoint invariance of
C
,
C(U_{0}U^{\dagger})\leqslant C(U_{0})+C(U^{\dagger})=C(U_{0})+C(U)\leqslant r+r^{\prime}
, so
Q^{\prime}\in\mathsf{M}^{r+r^{\prime}}
.
Moreover,
\tr({Q^{\prime}[U\rho U^{\dagger}]})=\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta
.
Therefore,
Q^{\prime}
is a candidate for the
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r+r^{\prime}},\,{\eta}}(U\rho U^{\dagger}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}U\Gamma U^{\dagger})
optimization, so

\displaystyle e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r+r^{\prime}},\,{\eta}}(U\rho U^{\dagger}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}U\Gamma U^{\dagger})}\leqslant\frac{\tr\big({Q^{\prime}\big[{U\Gamma U^{\dagger}}\big]}\big)}{\tr\big({Q^{\prime}\big[{U\rho U^{\dagger}}\big]}\big)}=\frac{\tr({Q\Gamma})}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)}\leqslant e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)}+\zeta\ ,

(121)

which implies
(
120a
)
, since
\zeta
is arbitrary.
∎

For composite systems, one can bound the complexity (relative) entropy in terms of hypothesis-testing (relative) entropies on each subsystem, if
\mathsf{M}^{r}
contains only tensor-product POVM effects.
In most cases,
\mathsf{M}^{r}
contains nonlocal effects for all
r>0
; in such cases, only
\mathsf{M}^{r=0}
may consist solely of tensor-product effects.
Importantly, the set
\mathsf{M}^{r=0}
defined in
(
10
)
, for
n
qubits, contains only tensor-product effects.

Proposition 32

(Upper bound by hypothesis-testing relative entropies of subsystems)
.

Let
S
denote a composition of
N
quantum subsystems:
S=S_{1}S_{2}\ldots S_{N}
.
Let
\rho_{S}
denote any subnormalized state.
For all
i=1,2,\ldots,N
, let
\Gamma_{S_{i}}
denote any positive-semidefinite operator.
Let
\Gamma_{S}\coloneqq\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}\Gamma_{S_{i}}
.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho_{S})]
.
Suppose that
\mathsf{M}^{r}_{S}
contains only tensor-product POVM effects: every
Q_{S}\in\mathsf{M}^{r}_{S}
is of the form
\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}Q_{S_{i}}
. Each
Q_{S_{i}}
is an effect on
S_{i}
.
It holds that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{S}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma_{S})

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{i=1}^{N}D_{\mathrm{H}}^{\eta}(\rho_{S_{i}}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma_{S_{i}})

(122a)

\displaystyle\text{and}\;\;\;H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho_{S}-NoValue-)

\displaystyle\geqslant\sum_{i=1}^{N}H_{\mathrm{H}}^{\eta}-NoValue-(\rho_{S_{i}}-NoValue-)\ .

(122b)

Proof.

Consider any
\zeta>0
.
There exists a
Q_{S}=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}Q_{S_{i}}\in\mathsf{M}^{r}_{S}
, with each
Q_{S_{i}}
an effect on
S_{i}
, such that
\tr\left({Q_{S}\rho_{S}}\right)\geqslant\eta
and
\tr\left({Q_{S}\Gamma_{S}}\right)/\tr\left({Q_{S}\rho_{S}}\right)\leqslant\exp\bm{(}-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{S}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma_{S})\bm{)}+\zeta
.
For all
i=1,2,\ldots,N
, let
Q_{S}^{(i)}\coloneqq\left({\mathds{1}_{S_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathds{1}_{S_{i-1}}}\right)\otimes Q_{S_{i}}\otimes\left({\mathds{1}_{S_{i+1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathds{1}_{S_{N}}}\right)
.
For all
i
,
Q_{S}^{(i)}\geqslant Q_{S}
, since
\mathds{1}_{S_{j}}\geqslant Q_{S_{j}}
for all
j\neq i
;
consequently,
\tr\left({Q_{S_{i}}\,\rho_{S_{i}}}\right)=\tr\big({Q_{S}^{(i)}\,\rho_{S}}\big)\geqslant\tr\left({Q_{S}\,\rho_{S}}\right)\geqslant\eta
.
Thus, for all
i
,
Q_{S_{i}}
is a candidate for the
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{\eta}(\rho_{S_{i}}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma_{S_{i}})
optimization, so
\exp\bm{(}-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{\eta}(\rho_{S_{i}}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma_{S_{i}})\bm{)}\leqslant\tr\big({Q_{S_{i}}\Gamma_{S_{i}}}\big)
.
Hence,

\displaystyle e^{-\sum_{i=1}^{N}D_{\mathrm{H}}^{\eta}(\rho_{S_{i}}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma_{S_{i}})}\leqslant\prod_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\tr\left({Q_{S_{i}}\Gamma_{S_{i}}}\right)}{\tr\left({Q_{S_{i}}\rho_{S_{i}}}\right)}\leqslant\prod_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\tr\left({Q_{S_{i}}\Gamma_{S_{i}}}\right)}{\tr\left({Q_{S}\rho_{S}}\right)}=\frac{\tr\left({Q_{S}\Gamma_{S}}\right)}{\tr\left({Q_{S}\rho_{S}}\right)}\leqslant e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{S}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma_{S})}+\zeta\ ,

(123)

which implies
(
122a
)
, since
\zeta
is arbitrary.
∎

D.3
Complexity relative entropy and hypothesis testing

We prove
Section

II.3
of the main text, quantifying the type I and type II errors of a hypothesis test that involves limited-complexity measurement effects.

Proposition 33

(Hypothesis testing with complexity limitations)
.

Let
\rho
and
\sigma
denote any quantum states.
Let
r\geqslant 0
,
\eta\in(0,1]
, and
\delta\in(0,1]
.
The following statements are equivalent:

(i)

For all
\zeta>0
, there exist a
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
and a
q\in[\eta,1]
such that
\tr({[qQ]\rho})=\eta
and
\tr({[qQ]\sigma})\leqslant\delta+\zeta
.

(ii)

It holds that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)\geqslant-\log\left({\frac{\delta}{\eta}}\right)\ .

(124)

Consequently, there exist a
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
and a
q\in(0,1]
such that
\tr({[qQ]\rho})=\eta
and
\tr({[qQ]\sigma})<\delta
if and only if

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)>-\log\left({\frac{\delta}{\eta}}\right)\ .

(125)

Corollary 34
.

Let
r\geqslant 0
.
If
\mathsf{M}^{r}
is compact, then statements (i) and (ii) in
Section

D.3
are equivalent to

(iii)

There exist a
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
and a
q\in[\eta,1]
such that
\tr({[qQ]\rho})=\eta
and
\tr({[qQ]\sigma})\leqslant\delta
.

In particular, suppose that
\mathsf{P}
is a set of simple POVM effects (
Section

B.4
) that is compact, and suppose that
C_{\mathcal{G}}
is a circuit-superoperator-complexity measure (
Section

B.1
) associated with a compact gate set
\mathcal{G}
.
Then
\mathsf{M}^{r}\left({\mathsf{P},C_{\mathcal{G}}}\right)
(
Section

B.4
) is compact.

In particular, the sets
\mathsf{M}^{r}
defined in
(
11
)
are compact.

Proof of
subsection D.3
.

To prove the first part of the proposition, consider any
\zeta>0
.
Suppose there exist a
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
and a
q\in[\eta,1]
such that
\tr({[qQ]\rho})=\eta
and
\tr({[qQ]\sigma})\leqslant\delta+\zeta
.
Then
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\tr({[qQ]\rho})=\eta
, and

\displaystyle e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)}\leqslant\frac{\tr\left({Q\sigma}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)}=\frac{\tr\left({[qQ]\sigma}\right)}{\tr\left({[qQ]\rho}\right)}\leqslant\frac{\delta+\zeta}{\eta}\ .

(126)

Therefore,
e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)}\leqslant\delta/\eta
, since
\zeta
is arbitrary.
Conversely, suppose that
\exp\bm{(}-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)\bm{)}\leqslant\delta/\eta
.
Consider again any
\zeta>0
.
There exists a
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
such that
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta
and
\tr({Q\sigma})/\tr({Q\rho})\leqslant(\delta+\zeta)/\eta
.
Let
q\coloneqq\eta/\tr({Q\rho})
.

q\in[\eta,1]
, since
\eta\leqslant\tr({Q\rho})\leqslant 1
.
Then
\tr({[qQ]\rho})=\eta
, and

\displaystyle\tr({[qQ]\sigma})=\tr({[qQ]\rho})\cdot\frac{\tr\left({Q\sigma}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)}\leqslant\tr({[qQ]\rho})\cdot\frac{\delta+\zeta}{\eta}=\delta+\zeta\ .

(127)

We now prove the second part of the proposition.
Suppose there exist a
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
and a
q\in(0,1]
such that
\tr({[qQ]\rho})=\eta
and
\tr({[qQ]\sigma})<\delta
.
Let
\delta^{\prime}\coloneqq\tr({[qQ]\sigma})<\delta
.

Q
,
q
, and
\delta^{\prime}
satisfy the conditions equivalent to
(
124
)
, so
\exp\bm{(}-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)\bm{)}\leqslant\delta^{\prime}/\eta<\delta/\eta
.
Conversely, suppose that
\exp\bm{(}-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)\bm{)}<\delta/\eta
.
Let
\delta^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq\eta\exp\bm{(}-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)\bm{)}<\delta
.
Then
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)\leqslant-\log(\delta^{\prime\prime}/\eta)
, as in
(
124
)
, so there exist
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
and
q\in(0,1]
such that
\tr({[qQ]\rho})=\eta
and
\tr({[qQ]\sigma})\leqslant\delta^{\prime\prime}+\zeta
.
By considering
\zeta
sufficiently small such that
\delta^{\prime\prime}+\zeta<\delta
, one obtains
\tr({[qQ]\sigma})<\delta
.
∎

Proof of
subsection D.3
.

The implication
\text{(iii)}\Rightarrow\text{(i)}
is straightforward; we need to show that
\text{(i)}\Rightarrow\text{(iii)}
.
Assume (i).
Consider the continuous functions
f,g:[\eta,1]\times\mathsf{M}^{r}\to[0,1]
defined as
f(q,Q)\coloneqq\tr({[qQ]\rho})
and
g(q,Q)\coloneqq\tr({[qQ]\sigma})
.
Let
A
denote the preimage of
\eta
under
f
:
A=f^{-1}(\{{\eta}\})
.

A
is nonempty, since, by assumption, (i) holds.

A
is closed, since
f
is continuous and
\{{\eta}\}
is closed.
Therefore,
A
is compact, being a closed subspace of the compact set
[\eta,1]\times\mathsf{M}^{r}
.
Let

\displaystyle\nu\coloneqq\inf\left\{{g(A)}\right\}=\inf\left\{{\tr\left({[qQ]\sigma}\right)\,:\;(q,Q)\in A}\right\}\ .

(128)

g(A)
is compact, since it is the image of a compact set under a continuous function.
Therefore, the infimum is attained, and there exists a
(q,Q)\in A
such that
g(q,Q)=\nu
.
Since (i) holds,
\nu\leqslant\delta+\zeta
for all
\zeta>0
; hence,
\nu\leqslant\delta
.
Thus, there exists
(q,Q)\in A
such that
g(q,Q)\leqslant\delta
, so (iii) holds.

Finally, suppose that
\mathsf{P}
and
\mathcal{G}
are compact.
Consider the continuous function
h(P,\mathcal{E}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{E}_{r})\coloneqq({\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\dagger}\cdots\mathcal{E}_{r}^{\dagger}})({P})
.

\mathsf{M}^{r}(\mathsf{P},C_{\mathcal{G}})
is compact, since it is the image of a compact set under a continuous function.
∎

D.4
Complexity (relative) entropy and state-complexity measures

The complexity entropy relates to pure-state complexity as follows.

Proposition 35

(Relation between complexity entropy and pure-state complexity)
.

Let
\mathsf{P}
denote any set of simple POVM effects (
Section

B.4
); and
C
, any adjoint-invariant unitary-complexity measure (
Section

B.1
).
Let
r\geqslant 0
, and let
\mathsf{M}^{r}=\mathsf{M}^{r}(\mathsf{P},C)
denote the POVM-effect-complexity set defined in
(
87
)
.
Let
\delta\geqslant 0
, and let
\lvert{\psi_{0}}\rangle
denote any pure reference state such that
\psi_{0}\in\mathsf{P}
.
Let
C^{\psi_{0},\delta}
denote the
\delta
-approximate-pure-state-complexity measure with respect to
C
and
\psi_{0}
(
Section

B.2
).
Let
\lvert{\psi}\rangle
denote any pure state.
The following statements hold:

1.

Suppose that
\delta<1
and
r>C^{\psi_{0},\,\delta}(\psi)
.
Then

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{1-\delta^{2}}}-NoValue-(\psi -NoValue-)\leqslant\log\left({\frac{1}{1-\delta^{2}}}\right)\ .

(129)

2.

Let
r_{0}\geqslant 0
and
\epsilon\in[0,1)
.
Suppose that
H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{1-\epsilon^{2}}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\leqslant\log\left({1/\left[{1-\epsilon^{2}}\right]}\right)
.
Then

\displaystyle C^{\psi_{0},\,\delta}(\psi)

\displaystyle\leqslant r+r_{0}\ ,

(130)

where

\displaystyle\delta

\displaystyle=\epsilon+\sqrt{\alpha\epsilon^{2}+(1-\alpha)}\ ;

\displaystyle\alpha\

\displaystyle\coloneqq\ \lim_{\zeta\to 0^{+}}\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathsf{P}\\
\frac{\tr({P^{2}})}{[\tr({P})]^{2}}\geqslant 1-8\epsilon^{2}-\zeta\end{subarray}}\;\left\{{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{0}:\\
\,C(U_{0})\leqslant r_{0}\end{subarray}}\;\left\{{\langle{\psi_{0}}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{U_{0}PU_{0}^{\dagger}}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{\psi_{0}}\rangle}\right\}}\right\}\ .

(131)

The error parameter
\alpha
is necessary for the second statement of the proposition.

\alpha
assumes simple values for suitable choices of
\mathsf{P}
,
C
,
\lvert{\psi_{0}}\rangle
, and
r_{0}
.
For instance, consider an
n
-qubit system, and let
\lvert{\psi_{0}}\rangle=\lvert{0^{n}}\rangle
.
Suppose that
\mathsf{P}
equals the set of single-qubit projectors in
(
10
)
.
For every
P\in\mathsf{P}
, the supremum in
(
131
)
achieves its maximum value with
U_{0}=\mathds{1}
:
\langle{0^{n}}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{U_{0}PU_{0}^{\dagger}}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{0^{n}}\rangle=\langle{0^{n}}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{0^{n}}\rangle=1
.
[For any
C
and any
r_{0}
,
C(\mathds{1})=0\leqslant r_{0}
.]
Having unit purity,
P=\lvert{0^{n}}\rangle\mkern-1.8mu\langle{0^{n}}\rvert
always satisfies the constraint on the infimum in
(
131
)
, so
\alpha=1
, and
\delta=2\epsilon
.
Now, suppose that
\mathsf{P}
equals the set of tensor products of all single-qubit projectors.
Suppose further that
C
assigns the value 1 (0) to every single-qubit unitary and that
r_{0}=n
(
r_{0}=0
).
Then, for all
P\in\mathsf{P}
, there exists a tensor product
U_{0}
of single-qubit unitaries such that
\langle{\psi_{0}}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{U_{0}PU_{0}^{\dagger}}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{\psi_{0}}\rangle=1
.

U_{0}
satisfies
C(U_{0})\leqslant r_{0}
.
Having unit purity, every rank-1 projector in
\mathsf{P}
always satisfies the constraint on the infimum in
(
131
)
; so, again,
\alpha=1
, and
\delta=2\epsilon
.

We now relate the complexity relative entropy to the strong complexity of Ref.
Brandão et al. 2021
.
Let

\displaystyle\beta^{r}({\rho,\sigma})\coloneqq\max_{M\in\mathsf{M}^{r}}\left\{{\left\lvert{\tr\left({M\left[{\rho-\sigma}\right]}\right)}\right\rvert}\right\}\ .

(132)

Let
\delta\geqslant 0
, and let
\lvert{\psi}\rangle
denote a pure state in a Hilbert space of dimensionality
d
.
The
strong complexity
of
\lvert{\psi}\rangle
is defined as

\displaystyle C_{\textup{strong}}^{\delta}(\lvert{\psi}\rangle)\coloneqq\inf\left\{{r\geqslant 0\,:\;\beta^{r}(\psi,\pi)\geqslant 1-\frac{1}{d}-\delta}\right\}\ .

(133)

The
\pi\coloneqq\mathds{1}/d
denotes the maximally mixed state.
Our definition of
\beta^{r}(\psi,\pi)
differs slightly from that in Ref.
Brandão et al. 2021
, wherein
\mathsf{M}^{r}
can be defined using an auxiliary system.

The following proposition presents a bound relating the complexity entropy and the strong complexity.

Proposition 36

(Complexity entropy and strong complexity)
.

Let
\rho
and
\sigma
denote any quantum states.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,1]
.
It holds that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)\leqslant-\log\left({1-\frac{\beta^{r}({\rho,\sigma})}{\eta}}\right)=\frac{\beta^{r}({\rho,\sigma})}{\eta}+O\left[{\left({\frac{\beta^{r}({\rho,\sigma})}{\eta}}\right)^{2}}\right]\ .

(134)

Furthermore, let
\delta\geqslant 0
, and let
\lvert{\psi}\rangle
denote any pure state in a Hilbert space of dimensionality
d
.
Suppose that
r<C_{\textup{strong}}^{\delta}(\lvert{\psi}\rangle)
and
\eta>1-d^{-1}-\delta
.
Then

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\psi -NoValue-)

\displaystyle>\log(d)-\log\left({\frac{1}{1-c}}\right)\ ;

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1-d^{-1}-\delta}{\eta}<1\ .

(135)

We now prove
Sections

D.4
and
D.4
.

Proof of
subsection D.4
.

We prove the first statement.
Let
\zeta\coloneqq r-C^{\psi_{0},\,\delta}(\psi)>0
.
There exists a unitary
U
such that
\frac{1}{2}\lVert{U\psi_{0}U^{\dagger}-\psi}\rVert_{1}\leqslant\delta
and
C(U)\leqslant r=C^{\psi_{0},\,\delta}(\psi)+\zeta
.
Let
Q\coloneqq U\psi_{0}U^{\dagger}
.

Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
, since
\psi_{0}\in\mathsf{P}
and
C(U^{\dagger})=C(U)\leqslant r
, by the adjoint invariance of
C
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\tr\left({Q\psi}\right)=\tr\left({U\psi_{0}U^{\dagger}\psi}\right)=\lvert{\langle{\psi}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{U}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{\psi_{0}}\rangle}\rvert^{2}=1-\left({\frac{1}{2}\lVert{U\psi_{0}U^{\dagger}-\psi}\rVert_{1}}\right)^{2}\geqslant 1-\delta^{2}\ .

(136)

The last equality expresses a general relation for the trace norm
BookNielsenChuang2000
:
for any two pure states
\lvert{\phi}\rangle
and
\lvert{\varphi}\rangle
,
\frac{1}{2}\lVert{\phi-\varphi}\rVert_{1}=\sqrt{1-\lvert{\langle{\varphi}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{\phi}\rangle}\rvert^{2}}
.
Thus,
Q
is a candidate for the
H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{1-\delta^{2}}}-NoValue-(\psi -NoValue-)
optimization, so

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{1-\delta^{2}}}-NoValue-(\psi -NoValue-)\leqslant\log\left({\frac{\tr\left({Q}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\psi}\right)}}\right)\leqslant\log\left({\frac{1}{1-\delta^{2}}}\right)\ .

(137)

We now prove the second statement.
Suppose that
H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{1-\epsilon^{2}}}-NoValue-(\psi -NoValue-)\leqslant\log\left({1/\left[{1-\epsilon^{2}}\right]}\right)
.
Let
\zeta>0
.
There exists a
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
such that
\tr({Q\psi})\geqslant 1-\epsilon^{2}
and

\displaystyle\frac{\tr({Q})}{\tr({Q\psi})}\leqslant e^{H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{1-\epsilon^{2}}}-NoValue-(\psi -NoValue-)}+\zeta\leqslant\frac{1}{1-\epsilon^{2}}+\zeta\ .

(138)

Q=U^{\dagger}PU
for some effect
P\in\mathsf{P}
and for some unitary
U
satisfying
C(U)\leqslant r
.
Let
\lvert{\psi^{\prime}}\rangle\coloneqq U\lvert{\psi}\rangle
.
It holds that

\displaystyle F^{2}\left({\psi^{\prime},\frac{P}{\tr(P)}}\right)=\langle{\psi^{\prime}}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{\frac{P}{\tr(P)}}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{\psi^{\prime}}\rangle=\frac{\tr(P\psi^{\prime})}{\tr(P)}=\left[{\frac{\tr({Q})}{\tr({Q\psi})}}\right]^{-1}\geqslant 1-\epsilon^{2}-\zeta_{1}\ .

(139)

Here and in the following, each
\zeta_{i}\equiv\zeta_{i}(\zeta)>0
is an error parameter such that
\lim_{\zeta\to 0^{+}}\zeta_{i}=0
.
By a Fuchs-van de Graaf inequality
Fuchs and van de Graaf 1999
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\bigg\lVert{\psi^{\prime}-\frac{P}{\tr(P)}}\bigg\rVert_{1}\leqslant\sqrt{1-F^{2}\left({\psi^{\prime},\frac{P}{\tr(P)}}\right)}\leqslant\sqrt{\epsilon^{2}+\zeta_{1}}\ .

(140)

Let

\displaystyle\alpha_{P}\coloneqq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{0}:\\
\,C(U_{0})\leqslant r_{0}\end{subarray}}\left\{{\langle{\psi_{0}}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{U_{0}PU_{0}^{\dagger}}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{\psi_{0}}\rangle}\right\}\ .

(141)

There exists a unitary
U_{0}
such that
C(U_{0})\leqslant r_{0}
and

\displaystyle\langle{\psi_{0}}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{U_{0}PU_{0}^{\dagger}}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{\psi_{0}}\rangle\geqslant\alpha_{P}-\zeta\ .

(142)

Consequently,

\displaystyle F^{2}\left({U_{0}^{\dagger}\psi_{0}U_{0},\frac{P}{\tr(P)}}\right)=\langle{\psi_{0}}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{U_{0}\,\frac{P}{\tr(P)}\,U_{0}^{\dagger}}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{\psi_{0}}\rangle\geqslant\frac{\alpha_{P}-\zeta}{\tr({P})}\geqslant\left({\alpha_{P}-\zeta}\right)\left({1-\epsilon^{2}-\zeta_{1}}\right)=\alpha_{P}\left({1-\epsilon^{2}}\right)-\zeta_{2}\ .

(143)

The second inequality follows from
(
139
)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tr({P})}\geqslant\frac{\tr({P\psi^{\prime}})}{\tr({P})}\geqslant 1-\epsilon^{2}-\zeta_{1}\ .

(144)

The largest eigenvalue of
P
,
\lVert{P}\rVert
, satisfies
\lVert{P}\rVert\geqslant\langle{\psi^{\prime}}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{P}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{\psi^{\prime}}\rangle=\langle{\psi}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{Q}\mkern 1.5mu|\mkern 1.5mu{\psi}\rangle\geqslant 1-\epsilon^{2}
, so
\tr\left({P^{2}}\right)\geqslant\lVert{P^{2}}\rVert=\lVert{P}\rVert^{2}\geqslant\left({1-\epsilon^{2}}\right)^{2}
, because
P
is positive-semidefinite.
Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{\tr\big({P^{2}}\big)}{\big[{\tr(P)}\big]^{2}}\geqslant\left({1-\epsilon^{2}}\right)^{2}\left({1-\epsilon^{2}-\zeta_{1}}\right)^{2}=\left({1-\epsilon^{2}}\right)^{4}-\zeta_{3}\geqslant 1-4\epsilon^{2}-4\left({\epsilon^{2}}\right)^{3}-\zeta_{3}\geqslant 1-8\epsilon^{2}-\zeta_{3}\ ,

(145)

so

\displaystyle\alpha_{P}\ \geqslant\ \alpha_{\zeta_{3}}\coloneqq\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathsf{P}\\
\frac{\tr(P^{2})}{[\tr(P)]^{2}}\geqslant 1-8\epsilon^{2}-\zeta_{3}\end{subarray}}\left\{{\alpha_{P}}\right\}\ .

(146)

Thus,

\displaystyle F^{2}\left({U_{0}^{\dagger}\psi_{0}U_{0},\frac{P}{\tr(P)}}\right)\geqslant\alpha_{\zeta_{3}}\left({1-\epsilon^{2}}\right)-\zeta_{2}\ .

(147)

By a Fuchs-van de Graaf inequality,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\bigg\lVert{U_{0}^{\dagger}\psi_{0}U_{0}-\frac{P}{\tr(P)}}\bigg\rVert_{1}\leqslant\sqrt{1-F^{2}\left({U_{0}^{\dagger}\psi_{0}U_{0},\frac{P}{\tr(P)}}\right)}\leqslant\sqrt{1-\alpha_{\zeta_{3}}\left({1-\epsilon^{2}}\right)-\zeta_{2}}\ .

(148)

Since the trace norm is unitarily invariant and obeys the triangle inequality,
(
140
)
and
(
148
)
imply that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big\lVert{\psi-U^{\dagger}U_{0}^{\dagger}\psi_{0}U_{0}U^{\dagger}}\big\rVert_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\big\lVert{U\psi U^{\dagger}-U_{0}^{\dagger}\psi_{0}U_{0}}\big\rVert_{1}\leqslant\sqrt{\epsilon^{2}+\zeta_{1}}+\sqrt{1-\alpha_{\zeta_{3}}\left({1-\epsilon^{2}}\right)-\zeta_{2}}\eqqcolon\delta_{\zeta}\ .

(149)

By the adjoint invariance of
C
,
C\big({U^{\dagger}U_{0}^{\dagger}}\big)\leqslant C\big({U^{\dagger}}\big)+C\big({U_{0}^{\dagger}}\big)=C\left({U}\right)+C\left({U_{0}}\right)\leqslant r+r_{0}
, so

\displaystyle C^{\psi_{0},\,\delta_{\zeta}}(\psi)\leqslant r+r_{0}\ .

(150)

Since
\lim_{\zeta\to 0^{+}}\delta_{\zeta}=\delta
and since
C
is right-continuous [as per
(
76
)
],

\displaystyle C^{\psi_{0},\,\delta}(\psi)=\lim_{\zeta\to 0^{+}}C^{\psi_{0},\,\delta_{\zeta}}(\psi)\leqslant r+r_{0}\ .

(151)

∎

Proof of
subsection D.4
.

Consider any
\zeta>0
.
There exists a
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
such that
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta
and
\tr({Q\sigma})/\tr({Q\rho})\leqslant\exp\bm{(}-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)\bm{)}+\zeta
.
It holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\tr\left({Q\sigma}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)}=1-\frac{\tr\left({Q[\rho-\sigma]}\right)}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)}\geqslant 1-\frac{\beta^{r}({\rho,\sigma})}{\tr\left({Q\rho}\right)}\geqslant 1-\frac{\beta^{r}({\rho,\sigma})}{\eta}\ ,

(152)

so

\displaystyle e^{-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)}\geqslant 1-\frac{\beta^{r}({\rho,\sigma})}{\eta}+\zeta\ ,

(153)

which implies
(
134
)
, since
\zeta
is arbitrary.

We now prove
(
135
)
.
Suppose that
r<C_{\textup{strong}}^{\delta}(\lvert{\psi}\rangle)
and
\eta>1-d^{-1}-\delta
.

r<C_{\textup{strong}}^{\delta}(\lvert{\psi}\rangle)
implies that
\beta^{r}(\psi,\pi)<1-d^{-1}-\delta<\eta
, so

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\psi -NoValue-)=\log({d})-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\psi\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\pi)

\displaystyle\geqslant\log({d})+\log\left({1-\frac{\beta^{r}(\psi,\pi)}{\eta}}\right)

\displaystyle>\log({d})+\log\left({1-\frac{1-d^{-1}-\delta}{\eta}}\right)=\log({d})-\log\left({\frac{1}{1-c}}\right)\ .

(154)

∎

D.5
Reduced complexity (relative) entropy and related quantities

D.5.1
Definition of the reduced complexity (relative) entropy

Some situations call for a variant of the complexity (relative) entropy—a variant that lacks the denominator in
(
100
)
[in
(
99
)
].
We call these variants the
reduced complexity relative entropy
and the
reduced complexity entropy
.
The variants lack a desirable property possessed by the original versions:
the reduced complexity (relative) entropy may diverge as
\eta\to 0
, whereas the complexity (relative) entropy will not whenever
\Gamma
is positive-definite (see
Section

D.2
).
Nevertheless, the variants bear simpler definitions than the originals and more readily facilitate certain technical proofs.
In any case, the reduced complexity (relative) entropy and the complexity (relative) entropy differ by at most
\log(1/\eta)
(
Section

D.5.1
).
Therefore, the quantities are interchangeable if the error tolerance is insignificant:
\eta\approx 1
.

Definition 37

(Reduced complexity relative entropy)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state; and
\Gamma
, any positive-semidefinite operator.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr({\rho})]
.
Here,
\rho
,
\Gamma
, and every
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
act on the same Hilbert space.
The
reduced complexity-restricted hypothesis-testing relative entropy
, or simply the
reduced complexity relative entropy
, is

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)\coloneqq-\log\;\left({\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}\\
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta\end{subarray}}\left\{{\tr\left({Q\Gamma}\right)}\right\}}\right)\ .

(155)

Definition 38

(Reduced complexity entropy)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
.
The
reduced complexity-restricted hypothesis-testing entropy
, or simply the
reduced complexity entropy
, is

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)

\displaystyle\coloneqq-D_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\mathds{1})=\log\;\left({\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}\\
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta\end{subarray}}\left\{{\tr\left({Q}\right)}\right\}}\right)\ .

(156)

By the constraint
\eta\leqslant\tr(Q\rho)\leqslant 1
, the reduced complexity (relative) entropy differs from the complexity (relative) entropy by at most
\log(1/\eta)
:

Proposition 39

(Difference between
D_{\mathrm{h}}^{r,\,\eta}
and
D_{\mathrm{H}}^{r,\,\eta}
)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state; and
\Gamma
, any positive-semidefinite operator.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
.
It holds that

\displaystyle 0\leqslant D_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)

\displaystyle\leqslant\log\left({\frac{1}{\eta}}\right)

(157a)

\displaystyle\text{and}\;\;\;0\leqslant H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)-H_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)

\displaystyle\leqslant\log\left({\frac{1}{\eta}}\right)\ .

(157b)

If
\rho
is normalized,
D_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta=1}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)=D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta=1}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
, and
H_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta=1}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)=H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta=1}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)
.

Proof.

The denominator
\tr(Q\rho)
in
Section

D.1
is constrained to
\eta\leqslant\tr(Q\rho)\leqslant 1
, leading to the claimed bounds.
The second part of the proposition follows by setting
\eta=1
in the bounds.
∎

The reduced complexity (relative) entropy also obeys bounds similar to those in
Section

D.2
.

Proposition 40

(General bounds for
D_{\mathrm{h}}^{r,\,\eta}
)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state, and
\Gamma
any positive-semidefinite operator, that act on a Hilbert space of dimensionality
d
.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
.
It holds that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)

\displaystyle\geqslant-\log\bm{(}\tr({\Gamma})\bm{)}\ .

(158a)

Furthermore, if
\Gamma
is positive-definite (has full rank),

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)

\displaystyle\leqslant\log\left({\lVert{\Gamma^{-1}}\rVert}\right)+\log\bm{(}\tr({\rho})\bm{)}+\log\left({\frac{1}{\eta}}\right)\ .

(158b)

In particular,

\displaystyle-\log\bm{(}\tr({\rho})\bm{)}-\log\left({\frac{1}{\eta}}\right)\leqslant H_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\leqslant\log(d)\ .

(159)

Consequently, if
\rho
is normalized and
\Gamma
is positive-definite,

\displaystyle-\log\bm{(}\tr({\Gamma})\bm{)}\leqslant D_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)

\displaystyle\leqslant\log\left({\lVert{\Gamma^{-1}}\rVert}\right)+\log\left({\frac{1}{\eta}}\right)\ ,

(160a)

\displaystyle\text{and}\;\;\;-\log\left({\frac{1}{\eta}}\right)\leqslant H_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)

\displaystyle\leqslant\log(d)\ .

(160b)

Proof.

(
158a
)
follows because
\mathds{1}\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
is a candidate effect for the
D_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
optimization.

(
158b
)
follows from combining
(
102b
)
of
Section

D.2
with
(
157a
)
of
Section

D.5.1
.
∎
(
158a
)
implies that
D_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\sigma)\geqslant 0
for normalized states
\rho
and
\sigma
.

The reduced complexity (relative) entropy possesses several elementary properties of the complexity (relative) entropy.

Proposition 41

(Properties of
D_{\mathrm{h}}^{r,\,\eta}
)
.

The following propositions hold if, everywhere therein, one replaces the complexity (relative) entropy with the reduced complexity (relative) entropy:

Section

D.2
,
Section

D.2
,
Section

D.2
,
Section

D.2
,
Section

D.2
, and
Section

D.2
.

Proof.

Adapt the propositions’ proofs as appropriate.
∎In general, the reduced complexity (relative) entropy lacks the properties stated in
Section

D.2
and in
Section

D.2
.

Section

D.2
does not hold because

D_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\rho)>0
if
\mathsf{M}^{r}=\{\mathds{1}\}
and
\tr({\mathds{1}})>1
.

Section

D.2
does not hold because
H_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)
, but not
H_{\mathrm{H}}^{\eta}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)
, can be negative.

D.5.2
Complexity success probability

It is convenient to introduce a quantity closely related to the reduced complexity entropy.
We call this quantity the
complexity success probability
.
Given any subnormalized state
\rho
and any
r\geqslant 0
, we have defined the reduced complexity entropy as the infimum of
\log\bm{(}\tr({Q})\bm{)}
over all
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
, subject to a constraint (a lower bound) on
\tr({Q\rho})
, the probability of successfully identifying
\rho
.
Reciprocally, we define the complexity success probability as the supremum of
\tr({Q\rho})
over all
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
, subject to a constraint (an upper bound) on
\log\bm{(}\tr({Q})\bm{)}
.

Definition 42

(Complexity success probability)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state.
Let
r\geqslant 0
.
Let
m_{-}\coloneqq\inf_{\eta}\left\{{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)}\right\}
and
m_{+}\coloneqq\sup_{\eta}\left\{{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)}\right\}=H_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta=\tr({\rho})}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)
, with
\eta
ranging over
(0,\tr({\rho})]
.
Let
m\in[m_{-},m_{+}]
if
m_{-}
is the minimum of
\left\{{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)}\right\}_{\eta}
; otherwise, let
m\in(m_{-},m_{+}]
.
The
reduced-complexity-entropy success probability
, or simply the
complexity success probability
, is

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{m}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\coloneqq\sup\left\{{\eta\,:\;H_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\leqslant m}\right\}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}\\
\log\bm{(}\tr({Q})\bm{)}\leqslant m\end{subarray}}\left\{{\tr({Q\rho})}\right\}\ .

(161)

\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{m}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)
appears in the proof of
Appendix

F
(
Appendix

F
).

\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{m}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)
takes a simple form in the special case where
\mathsf{M}^{r}
is defined for
n
qubits, as in
(
11
)
.
Consider any candidate
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
.

Q=U^{\dagger}PU
for some unitary
U
and for some effect
P
that, up to a permutation of qubits, equals
\mathds{1}_{2}^{\otimes k_{Q}}\otimes\lvert{0^{n-k_{Q}}}\rangle\mkern-1.8mu\langle{0^{n-k_{Q}}}\rvert
, wherein
k_{Q}\coloneqq\log_{2}\bm{(}\tr({Q})\bm{)}
.

\log\bm{(}\tr({Q})\bm{)}\leqslant m
precisely if
k_{Q}\leqslant m/\log(2)
, which holds precisely if
k_{Q}\leqslant\lfloor{m/\log(2)}\rfloor
.
Moreover, if
k_{Q}<\lfloor{m/\log(2)}\rfloor
, then there exists a
\tilde{Q}\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
such that
k_{\tilde{Q}}=\lfloor{m/\log(2)}\rfloor
and
\tr({\tilde{Q}\rho})\geqslant\tr({Q\rho})
.

\big(
Indeed, let
\tilde{Q}\coloneqq U^{\dagger}\tilde{P}U
, wherein
\tilde{P}
, up to the aforementioned permutation, equals
\mathds{1}_{2}^{\otimes k_{\tilde{Q}}}\otimes\lvert{0^{n-k_{\tilde{Q}}}}\rangle\mkern-1.8mu\langle{0^{n-k_{\tilde{Q}}}}\rvert
.
\big)

Thus, we can replace the constraint
k_{Q}\leqslant\lfloor{m/\log(2)}\rfloor
with
k_{Q}=\lfloor{m/\log(2)}\rfloor
:

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{m}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}\\
\log_{2}\bm{(}\tr({Q})\bm{)}=\lfloor{m/\log(2)}\rfloor\end{subarray}}\left\{{\tr({Q\rho})}\right\}\ .

(162)

The complexity success probability monotonically increases as
r
increases and monotonically increases as
m
increases.

Proposition 43

(Monotonicity of
\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{m}}
in
r
and
m
)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state.
Let
r\geqslant 0
.
Let
m
and
m_{+}
be as in
Section

D.5.2
.

1.

For all
r^{\prime}\geqslant r
,
\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r^{\prime}},\,{m}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\geqslant\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{m}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)
.

2.

For all
m^{\prime}\in[m,m_{+}]
,
\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{m^{\prime}}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\geqslant\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{m}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)
.

Proof.

Consider the definition
(
161
)
of
\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{m}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)
.
Consider any
\eta\in(0,\tr({\rho})]
.
The monotonicity in
r
follows because
H_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\leqslant m
implies that
H_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r^{\prime}},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\leqslant m
, by
Section

D.2
(via
Section

D.5.1
).
The monotonicity in
m
follows because
H_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\leqslant m
implies that
H_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\leqslant m^{\prime}
.
∎

Moreover, the complexity success probability is convex.

Proposition 44

(Convexity of
\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{m}}
)
.

Let
\{{p_{k}}\}
denote any probability distribution; and
\{{\rho_{k}}\}
, any associated collection of
subnormalized states.
Let
r\geqslant 0
.
Let
m
be as in
Section

D.5.2
.
It holds that

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{m}}\left(\sum_{k}p_{k}\rho_{k}\right)\leqslant\sum_{k}p_{k}\,\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{m}}-NoValue-(\rho_{k}-NoValue-)\ .

(163)

Proof.

Consider any
\zeta>0
.
There exists a
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
such that
\log\bm{(}\tr({Q})\bm{)}\leqslant m
and

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{m}}\left(\sum_{k}p_{k}\rho_{k}\right)-\zeta\leqslant\tr\left({Q\left[{\sum_{k}p_{k}\rho_{k}}\right]}\right)=\sum_{k}p_{k}\,\tr({Q\rho_{k}})\leqslant\sum_{k}p_{k}\,\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{m}}-NoValue-(\rho_{k}-NoValue-)\ .

(164)

The second inequality holds because, for each
k
,
Q
is a candidate for the
\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{m}}-NoValue-(\rho_{k}-NoValue-)
optimization and thus satisfies
\tr({Q\rho_{k}})\leqslant\eta_{\mathrm{h}}^{{r},\,{m}}-NoValue-(\rho_{k}-NoValue-)
.

(
163
)
now follows because
\zeta
is arbitrary.
∎

D.5.3
Interrelating reduced complexity (relative) entropies under two computational models

Under certain conditions, a simple bound relates reduced complexity (relative) entropies defined with respect to different computational models.

Proposition 45

(Reduced complexity relative entropies under two computational models)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state; and
\Gamma
, any positive-semidefinite operator.
Let
\mathsf{P}
denote any set of simple POVM effects (
Section

B.4
), and let
C^{(1)}
and
C^{(2)}
denote superoperator-complexity measures (
Section

B.1
).
For each
i=1,2
, let
\big\{\mathsf{M}^{r,\,(i)}\coloneqq\mathsf{M}^{r}\big(\mathsf{P},C^{(i)}\big)\big\}
denote the family of POVM-effect-complexity sets defined by
(
87
)
.
Let
r\geqslant 0
,
\alpha>0
,
\epsilon\in[0,1]
, and
\eta\in(\epsilon,1]
.
Assume there exists a monotonically increasing function
f:\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
with the following property:
for every operation
\mathcal{E}
satisfying
C^{(1)}(\mathcal{E})<\infty
, there exists an operation
\mathcal{F}
such that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lVert{\mathcal{E}-\mathcal{F}}\rVert_{\diamond}\leqslant\epsilon\ ,\quad\mathcal{F}(\Gamma)\leqslant\alpha\mathcal{E}(\Gamma)\ ,\quad\text{and}\quad C^{(2)}(\mathcal{F})\leqslant f\bm{(}C^{(1)}(\mathcal{E})\bm{)}\ .

(165)

It holds that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{h},\,(2)}^{{f(r)},\,{\eta-\epsilon}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)\geqslant D_{\mathrm{h},\,(1)}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)-\log({\alpha})\ .

(166)

The reduced complexity relative entropies
D_{\mathrm{h},\,(1)}^{{r},\,{\eta}}
and
D_{\mathrm{h},\,(2)}^{{f(r)},\,{\eta-\epsilon}}
are defined with respect to
\mathsf{M}^{r,\,(1)}
and
\mathsf{M}^{f(r),\,(2)}
, respectively.

Corollary 46

(Reduced complexity entropies under two unitary computational models)
.

Let
\rho
denote any subnormalized state.
Let
\mathsf{P}
denote any set of simple POVM effects (
Section

B.4
), and let
C^{(1)}
and
C^{(2)}
denote unitary-complexity measures (
Section

B.1
).
For each
i=1,2
, let
\big\{\mathsf{M}^{r,\,(i)}\coloneqq\mathsf{M}^{r}\big(\mathsf{P},C^{(i)}\big)\big\}
denote the family of POVM-effect-complexity sets defined by
(
87
)
.
Let
r\geqslant 0
,
\epsilon\in[0,1]
, and
\eta\in(\epsilon,1]
.
Assume there exists a monotonically increasing function
f:\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
with the following property:
for every unitary
U
satisfying
C^{(1)}(U)<\infty
, there exists a unitary
V
such that

\displaystyle\lVert{U-V}\rVert\leqslant\epsilon\quad\text{and}\quad C^{(2)}(V)\leqslant f\bm{(}C^{(1)}(U)\bm{)}\ .

(167)

It holds that

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{h},\,(2)}^{{f(r)},\,{\eta-\epsilon}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\leqslant H_{\mathrm{h},\,(1)}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)\ .

(168)

The reduced complexity entropies
H_{\mathrm{h},\,(1)}^{{r},\,{\eta}}{}
and
H_{\mathrm{h},\,(2)}^{{f(r)},\,{\eta-\epsilon}}{}
are defined with respect to
\mathsf{M}^{r,\,(1)}
and
\mathsf{M}^{f(r),\,(2)}
, respectively.

Proof of
subsubsection D.5.3
.

Consider any
\zeta>0
.
There exists a
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r,\,(1)}
such that
D_{\mathrm{h},\,(1)}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)\leqslant-\log\bm{(}\tr({Q\Gamma})\bm{)}+\zeta
.

Q=\mathcal{E}^{\dagger}(P)
for some effect
P\in\mathsf{P}
and for some operation
\mathcal{E}
satisfying
C^{(1)}(\mathcal{E})\leqslant r
.
By assumption, there exists an operation
\mathcal{F}
satisfying the conditions
(
165
)
.
Hence,
C^{(2)}(\mathcal{F})\leqslant f\bm{(}C^{(1)}(\mathcal{E})\bm{)}\leqslant f(r)
.
The second inequality follows because
f
is monotonically increasing.
Let
Q^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{F}^{\dagger}(P)\in\mathsf{M}^{f(r),\,(2)}
.
It holds that

\displaystyle\tr({Q^{\prime}\rho})=\tr\bm{(}P\mathcal{F}(\rho)\bm{)}\geqslant\tr\bm{(}P\mathcal{E}(\rho)\bm{)}-\epsilon=\tr({Q\rho})-\epsilon\geqslant\eta-\epsilon\ .

(169)

The first inequality follows because
\tr\bm{(}P\mathcal{F}(\rho)\bm{)}\geqslant\tr\bm{(}P\mathcal{E}(\rho)\bm{)}-\frac{1}{2}\lVert{\mathcal{E}(\rho)-\mathcal{F}(\rho)}\rVert_{1}
, by Corollary 9.1.1 of Ref.
Wilde 2013
, and because
\frac{1}{2}\lVert{\mathcal{E}-\mathcal{F}}\rVert_{\diamond}\leqslant\epsilon
implies that
\frac{1}{2}\lVert{\mathcal{E}(\rho)-\mathcal{F}(\rho)}\rVert_{1}\leqslant\epsilon
, by
(
63
)
.
Therefore,
Q^{\prime}
is a candidate for the
D_{\mathrm{h},\,(2)}^{{f(r)},\,{\eta-\epsilon}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
optimization.
Moreover,
\tr({Q^{\prime}\Gamma})=\tr\bm{(}P\mathcal{F}(\Gamma)\bm{)}\leqslant\tr\bm{(}P\left[{\alpha\mathcal{E}(\Gamma)}\right]\bm{)}=\alpha\tr({Q\Gamma})
, so

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{h},\,(2)}^{{f(r)},\,{\eta-\epsilon}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)\geqslant-\log\bm{(}\tr({Q^{\prime}\Gamma})\bm{)}\geqslant-\log\bm{(}\tr({Q\Gamma})\bm{)}-\log({\alpha})\geqslant D_{\mathrm{h},\,(1)}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)-\log({\alpha})-\zeta\ ,

(170)

which implies
(
166
)
, since
\zeta
is arbitrary.
∎

Proof of
subsubsection D.5.3
.

(
168
)
follows from applying
Section

D.5.3
when
\Gamma=\mathds{1}
and
\alpha=1
.
We need to show only that the conditions
(
165
)
hold in this case.
Let
\Gamma=\mathds{1}
and
\alpha=1
.
Consider any operation
\mathcal{E}
satisfying
C^{(1)}(\mathcal{E})<\infty
.

\mathcal{E}
is a unitary operation, since
C^{(1)}
is a unitary-complexity measure.
Hence,
\mathcal{E}(\cdot)=U({\cdot})U^{\dagger}
for some unitary operator
U
satisfying
C^{(1)}(U)<\infty
.
By assumption, there exists a unitary operator
V
such that
\lVert{U-V}\rVert\leqslant\epsilon
and
C^{(2)}(V)\leqslant f\bm{(}C^{(1)}(U)\bm{)}
.
Let
\mathcal{F}(\cdot)\coloneqq V({\cdot})V^{\dagger}
.
By
Appendix

A
,
\frac{1}{2}\lVert{\mathcal{E}-\mathcal{F}}\rVert_{\diamond}\leqslant\lVert{U-V}\rVert\leqslant\epsilon
.
Also,
\mathcal{F}(\Gamma)\leqslant\alpha\mathcal{E}(\Gamma)
, since
\mathcal{F}(\mathds{1})=\mathds{1}=\mathcal{E}(\mathds{1})
.
Last,
C^{(2)}(\mathcal{F})\leqslant f\bm{(}C^{(1)}(\mathcal{E})\bm{)}
is a restatement of
C^{(2)}(V)\leqslant f\bm{(}C^{(1)}(U)\bm{)}
.
∎

D.6
Complexity conditional entropy

We here define the
complexity conditional entropy
in terms of the complexity relative entropy, just as one defines one-shot conditional entropies in terms of one-shot relative entropies
Tomamichel 2016
.
In
Section

V.7
, the complexity conditional entropy quantifies the maximally mixed qubits one can decouple from a reference system.

Definition 47

(Complexity conditional entropy)
.

Let
A
and
B
denote distinct quantum systems.
Let
\rho_{AB}
denote any subnormalized state of
AB
.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
.
The
complexity conditional entropy
of
A
conditioned on
B
is

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(A\mathclose{}\,|\,\mathopen{}B)_{\rho}\coloneqq-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{AB}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\mathds{1}_{A}\otimes\rho_{B})\ .

(171)

Proposition 48

(General bounds for the complexity conditional entropy)
.

Let
A
and
B
denote distinct quantum systems.
Let
\rho_{AB}
denote any subnormalized state of
AB
.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
.
It holds that

\displaystyle-\log(d_{A})\leqslant H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(A\mathclose{}\,|\,\mathopen{}B)_{\rho}\leqslant\log(d_{A})\ .

(172)

Equivalently,

\displaystyle 0\leqslant D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{AB}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\pi_{A}\otimes\rho_{B})\leqslant 2\log(d_{A})\ .

(173)

Proof.

H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(A\mathclose{}\,|\,\mathopen{}B)_{\rho}\leqslant\log(d_{A})
follows from applying
(
102a
)
in
Section

D.2
with
\Gamma=\mathds{1}_{A}\otimes\rho_{B}
:

\displaystyle-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{AB}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\mathds{1}_{A}\otimes\rho_{B})\leqslant\log\bm{(}\tr({\mathds{1}_{A}\otimes\rho_{B}})\bm{)}-\log\bm{(}\tr({\rho_{AB}})\bm{)}=\log\bm{(}d_{A}\tr({\rho_{B}})\bm{)}-\log\bm{(}\tr({\rho_{B}})\bm{)}=\log(d_{A})\ .

(174)

The first equality follows because
\tr(\rho_{AB})=\tr_{B}\bm{(}\tr_{A}(\rho_{AB})\bm{)}=\tr(\rho_{B})
.

H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(A\mathclose{}\,|\,\mathopen{}B)_{\rho}\geqslant-\log(d_{A})
follows from applying
Section

D.2
to the inequality
d_{A}^{-1}\rho_{AB}\leqslant\mathds{1}_{A}\otimes\rho_{B}
(
Appendix

A
):

\displaystyle-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{AB}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\mathds{1}_{A}\otimes\rho_{B})\geqslant-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{AB}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}d_{A}^{-1}\rho_{AB})=-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{AB}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\rho_{AB})-\log(d_{A})=-\log(d_{A})\ .

(175)

The first equality follows from
Section

D.2
and the second equality from
Section

D.2
.

Last,
(
172
)
and
(
173
)
are equivalent because
Section

D.2
implies that
H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(A\mathclose{}\,|\,\mathopen{}B)_{\rho}=\log(d_{A})-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{AB}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\pi_{A}\otimes\rho_{B})
:

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(A\mathclose{}\,|\,\mathopen{}B)_{\rho}=-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{AB}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\mathds{1}_{A}\otimes\rho_{B})=-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{AB}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}d_{A}\pi_{A}\otimes\rho_{B})=\log(d_{A})-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{AB}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\pi_{A}\otimes\rho_{B})\ .

(176)

∎

Proposition 49

(Strong subadditivity of the complexity conditional entropy)
.

Let
A
,
B
, and
C
denote distinct quantum systems.
Let
\rho_{ABC}
denote any subnormalized state of
ABC
.
Let
r\geqslant 0
and
\eta\in(0,\tr(\rho)]
.
It holds that

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(A\mathclose{}\,|\,\mathopen{}BC)_{\rho}\leqslant H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(A\mathclose{}\,|\,\mathopen{}B)_{\rho}\ .

(177)

Proof.

By
Section

D.2
, the complexity relative entropy never increases under a partial trace, so

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(A\mathclose{}\,|\,\mathopen{}BC)_{\rho}=-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{ABC}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\mathds{1}_{A}\otimes\rho_{BC})\leqslant-D_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho_{AB}\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\mathds{1}_{A}\otimes\rho_{B})=H_{\mathrm{H}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(A\mathclose{}\,|\,\mathopen{}B)_{\rho}\ .

(178)

∎

Appendix E
Thermodynamic erasure of qubits governed by a product Hamiltonian

Here, we generalize the arguments in
Section

III.1
to prove
Section

III.2
(
Section

III.2
).
Recall the theorem’s setting.
We consider a system of
n
noninteracting qubits.
Qubit
i
evolves under a Hamiltonian
H_{i}
that has zero ground-state energy,
H_{i}\lvert{0}\rangle_{i}=0
.
We fix an inverse temperature
\beta>0
.
Qubit
i
has the Gibbs-weight operator
\Gamma_{i}=e^{-\beta H_{i}}
; and the
n
-qubit system, the Gibbs-weight operator
\Gamma=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}=e^{-\beta\sum_{i}H_{i}}
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
denote a set of elementary computations on the
n
-qubit system.
Each computation in
\mathcal{T}
is a completely positive, trace-preserving map that sends
\Gamma
to itself and costs no work to implement.
After implementing elementary computations, one can apply
reset
operations to a selection
\mathcal{W}\subset\{{1,2,\ldots,n}\}
of qubits.
Each
reset
operation
\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{reset},i}
initializes qubit
i
in the state
\lvert{0}\rangle_{i}
and costs an amount of work
W_{\textsc{reset},i}=\beta^{-1}\log\bm{(}\tr({\Gamma_{i}})\bm{)}\geqslant 0
.
Consider a general protocol
\mathcal{E}
consisting of
r\geqslant 0
elementary computations followed by
reset
operations on the qubits of
\mathcal{W}
.

\mathcal{E}
has the form
\mathcal{E}=\left({\textstyle\prod_{i\in\mathcal{W}}\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{RESET},i}}\right)\mathcal{E}_{r}\cdots\mathcal{E}_{2}\mathcal{E}_{1}
.
Each
\mathcal{E}_{i}
is in
\mathcal{T}
, and the work cost
W(\mathcal{E})=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{W}}W_{\textsc{reset},i}=\beta^{-1}\log\bm{(}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{W}}\tr({\Gamma_{i}})\bm{)}
.

Theorem 50
.

Let
\rho
denote any quantum state of
n
qubits.
Let
\mathsf{P}
denote the set of simple POVM effects (
Section

B.4
) defined in
(
10
)
.
Let
C_{\mathcal{T}}
denote the circuit-complexity measure associated with
\mathcal{T}
(
Section

B.1
).
Let
r\geqslant 0
denote an integer, and let
\eta\in(0,1]
.
Let
\mathsf{M}^{r}=\mathsf{M}^{r}\left({\mathsf{P},C_{\mathcal{T}}}\right)
denote the POVM-effect-complexity set defined in
(
87
)
.
Let

\displaystyle W_{r}^{*}\coloneqq\min\left\{{W(\mathcal{E})\,:\ \mathcal{E}=\left({\textstyle\prod_{i\in\mathcal{W}}\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{RESET},i}}\right)\mathcal{E}_{r}\cdots\mathcal{E}_{2}\mathcal{E}_{1}\,,\;\mathcal{W}\subset\{{1,2,\ldots,n}\}\,,\;F^{2}\bm{(}\mathcal{E}(\rho),\lvert{0^{n}}\rangle\mkern-1.8mu\langle{0^{n}}\rvert\bm{)}\geqslant\eta}\right\}\ .

(179)

Each
\mathcal{E}_{i}
is in
\mathcal{T}
, and
F
denotes the fidelity between quantum states.
It holds that

\displaystyle\beta W_{r}^{*}=-D_{\mathrm{h},\,\mathcal{T}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)\ ,

(180)

wherein the reduced complexity relative entropy
D_{\mathrm{h},\,\mathcal{T}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}
(
Section

D.5.1
) is defined with respect to
\mathsf{M}^{r}
.
Consequently,

\displaystyle-D_{\mathrm{H},\,\mathcal{T}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)-\log\left({\frac{1}{\eta}}\right)\leqslant\beta W_{r}^{*}\leqslant-D_{\mathrm{H},\,\mathcal{T}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)\ .

(181)

Consider the special case where each Hamiltonian
H_{i}
is degenerate:
H_{i}=0
.
In this case,
\Gamma=\mathds{1}
.
By
(
180
)
,
\beta W_{r}^{*}
equals the reduced complexity entropy (
Section

D.5.1
):
\beta W_{r}^{*}=-D_{\mathrm{h},\,\mathcal{T}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\mathds{1})=H_{\mathrm{h},\,\mathcal{T}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}-NoValue-(\rho -NoValue-)
.
If, furthermore,
\mathcal{T}
contains only unitary operations, then
(
181
)
yields the bounds in
Section

III.1.1
(
Section

III.1
).

Proof of
Appendix E
.

We first prove that
\beta W_{r}^{*}\geqslant-D_{\mathrm{h},\,\mathcal{T}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
.
Consider any operation
\mathcal{E}
that achieves the minimum in
(
179
)
and thus satisfies
W(\mathcal{E})=W_{r}^{*}
.

\mathcal{E}=\left({\textstyle\prod_{i\in\mathcal{W}}\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{RESET},i}}\right)\mathcal{E}_{r}\cdots\mathcal{E}_{2}\mathcal{E}_{1}
for some subset
\mathcal{W}\subset\{{1,2,\ldots,n}\}
and some operations
\mathcal{E}_{i}\in\mathcal{T}
.
Moreover,
F^{2}\bm{(}\mathcal{E}(\rho),\lvert{0^{n}}\rangle\mkern-1.8mu\langle{0^{n}}\rvert\bm{)}\geqslant\eta
.
Let
P\coloneqq\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{W}}\otimes\lvert{0^{n-\lvert{\mathcal{W}}\rvert}}\rangle\mkern-1.8mu\langle{0^{n-\lvert{\mathcal{W}}\rvert}}\rvert_{\mathcal{W}^{\rm c}}\in\mathsf{P}
, wherein
\mathcal{W}^{\rm c}
denotes the set complement of
\mathcal{W}
.
Let
Q\coloneqq\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\dagger}\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\dagger}\cdots\mathcal{E}_{r}^{\dagger}({P})\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
.
It holds that

\displaystyle\tr({Q\rho})

\displaystyle=\tr\bm{(}P\,\mathcal{E}_{r}\cdots\mathcal{E}_{2}\mathcal{E}_{1}(\rho)\bm{)}=\tr\left\{{\lvert{0^{n-\lvert{\mathcal{W}}\rvert}}\rangle\mkern-1.8mu\langle{0^{n-\lvert{\mathcal{W}}\rvert}}\rvert_{\mathcal{W}^{\rm c}}\tr_{\mathcal{W}}\bm{(}\mathcal{E}_{r}\cdots\mathcal{E}_{2}\mathcal{E}_{1}(\rho)\bm{)}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\tr\bm{(}\lvert{0^{n}}\rangle\mkern-1.8mu\langle{0^{n}}\rvert\,\mathcal{E}(\rho)\bm{)}=F^{2}\bm{(}\mathcal{E}(\rho),\lvert{0^{n}}\rangle\mkern-1.8mu\langle{0^{n}}\rvert\bm{)}\ .

(182)

The third equality follows because
\mathcal{E}(\rho)=\lvert{0^{\lvert{\mathcal{W}}\rvert}}\rangle\mkern-1.8mu\langle{0^{\lvert{\mathcal{W}}\rvert}}\rvert_{\mathcal{W}}\otimes\tr_{\mathcal{W}}\bm{(}\mathcal{E}_{r}\cdots\mathcal{E}_{2}\mathcal{E}_{1}(\rho)\bm{)}
.
Hence,
\tr(Q\rho)\geqslant\eta
, so
Q
is a candidate for the
D_{\mathrm{H},\,\mathcal{T}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
optimization.
Furthermore,

\displaystyle\tr({Q\Gamma})=\tr\bm{(}P\,\mathcal{E}_{r}\cdots\mathcal{E}_{1}(\Gamma)\bm{)}=\tr({P\Gamma})=\tr\left({\Bigg[{\prod_{i\in\mathcal{W}}\Gamma_{i}}\Bigg]\otimes\lvert{0^{n-\lvert{\mathcal{W}}\rvert}}\rangle\mkern-1.8mu\langle{0^{n-\lvert{\mathcal{W}}\rvert}}\rvert_{\mathcal{W}^{\rm c}}}\right)=\prod_{i\in\mathcal{W}}\tr({\Gamma_{i}})=e^{\beta W(\mathcal{E})}\ .

(183)

The second equality follows because
\mathcal{E}_{r}\cdots\mathcal{E}_{2}\mathcal{E}_{1}(\Gamma)=\Gamma
, since each
\mathcal{E}_{i}
satisfies
\mathcal{E}_{i}(\Gamma)=\Gamma
.
Thus,
\beta W_{r}^{*}=\beta W(\mathcal{E})=\log\bm{(}\tr({Q\Gamma})\bm{)}\geqslant-D_{\mathrm{h},\,\mathcal{T}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
.

We now prove that
\beta W_{r}^{*}\leqslant-D_{\mathrm{h},\,\mathcal{T}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
.
Consider any
\zeta>0
.
There exists a
Q\in\mathsf{M}^{r}
such that
\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta
and
\log\bm{(}\tr({Q\Gamma})\bm{)}\leqslant-D_{\mathrm{h},\,\mathcal{T}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)+\zeta
.
Since
\mathsf{M}^{r}=\mathsf{M}^{r}\left({\mathsf{P},C_{\mathcal{T}}}\right)
,
Q=\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\dagger}\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\dagger}\cdots\mathcal{E}_{r}^{\dagger}(P)
for some operations
\mathcal{E}_{i}\in\mathcal{T}
and for some effect
P\in\mathsf{P}
, with
P=\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{W}}\otimes\lvert{0^{n-\lvert{\mathcal{W}}\rvert}}\rangle\mkern-1.8mu\langle{0^{n-\lvert{\mathcal{W}}\rvert}}\rvert_{\mathcal{W}^{\rm c}}
for some subset
\mathcal{W}\subset\{{1,2,\ldots,n}\}
.
Let
\mathcal{E}\coloneqq\left({\textstyle\prod_{i\in\mathcal{W}}\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{RESET},i}}\right)\mathcal{E}_{r}\cdots\mathcal{E}_{2}\mathcal{E}_{1}
.
As per
(
182
)
,
F^{2}\bm{(}\mathcal{E}(\rho),\lvert{0^{n}}\rangle\mkern-1.8mu\langle{0^{n}}\rvert\bm{)}=\tr({Q\rho})\geqslant\eta
, so
\mathcal{E}
is a candidate for the
W_{r}^{*}
optimization in
(
179
)
.
As per
(
183
)
,
\tr({Q\Gamma})=e^{\beta W(\mathcal{E})}
, so
\beta W_{r}^{*}\leqslant\beta W(\mathcal{E})=\log\bm{(}\tr({Q\Gamma})\bm{)}\leqslant-D_{\mathrm{h},\,\mathcal{T}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)+\zeta
.
Thus,
\beta W_{r}^{*}\leqslant-D_{\mathrm{h},\,\mathcal{T}}^{{r},\,{\eta}}(\rho\mathclose{}\,\|\,\mathopen{}\Gamma)
, since
\zeta
is arbitrary.
Finally,
(
181
)
follows from substituting
(
180
)
into
(
157a
)
.
∎

Appendix F
Evolution of the complexity entropy under random circuits

Here, we prove the bound
(
47
)
to complete the proof of
Section

V.4
(
Section

V.4
), which describes the complexity entropy’s evolution under random circuits.
Random circuits are often used as proxies for Hamiltonian quantum chaotic dynamics.
We adapt the proof of Theorem 8 in Ref.
Brandão et al. 2021
; the theorem describes the strong complexity’s growth under random circuits.

We consider a system of
n\geqslant 2
qubits.
Let
G
denote any set of two-qubit unitary gates:
G\subset\mathrm{SU}({4})
.
Let
\left\{{\mathsf{M}^{r}_{G}}\right\}
denote the family of POVM-effect-complexity sets (
Section

B.3
) defined by
(
11
)
, wherein the computational gate set
\mathcal{G}
